M19 → C04 "a repair answer that arrives after the playback was replaced is dropped" (FakeServer replies may now be
async); M20 → C14 "a stall while hls.js retries a 504 … lower quality" (the `!== 504` guard in `stallFailure` was equivalent — classify already makes a 504 T5 `segment_timeout` — and was removed; M20 now mutates that classify mapping); M22 → C10 "a media request answered 401/403
starts anew". D05 uses a realistic `frag`. E16 no longer reads source text: `createPlayer(load, make)` (new, in
`player/caps-fallback.ts`, used by the play screen) is tested with a rejecting caps loader — the controller is made
with the fallback profile and its start request carries it.

## Slice S6 — expo-video native probes (worktree `../streamarr-f10n`, branch `round-i/f10n`)

Status: in-progress (S6 + S7 code, jest, Android + iPhone + Apple TV live done 03:47–06:45; open: ladder step A, Android audio-focus/noisy live with sound). Nothing committed.

**What.** The patched expo-video reports what ExoPlayer and AVPlayer know, and the expo-video engine answers
`readHealth()` in the one `EngineHealth` shape, so the S5 watchdog now runs on Exo and AVPlayer too.

| Part | Where |
|---|---|
| **Android** (`patches/expo-video+57.0.5.patch`): failed items carry `errorCodeName`, `errorCode`, the HTTP status of the failing load (`InvalidResponseCodeException.responseCode` from the cause chain), the renderer's format `mimeType` and `renderer`; `readHealthAsync()` (main queue) returns video decoder counters (`renderedOutputBufferCount` → framesPresented, `droppedBufferCount`, decoded), audio decoder rendered buffers (audioProgress; 0 when tracks are known and no audio renderer runs), `audioUnderruns` (`onAudioUnderrun`), first rendered frame since the source (readyForDisplay), selected video/audio track, bandwidth meter estimate, `currentPosition`; zero frames when the tracks are known and no video track is selected (D16). `systemPlayback {paused, cause}` from `onPlayWhenReadyChanged`: becoming noisy → `headphones` (ExoPlayer now `setHandleAudioBecomingNoisy(true)`), audio-focus loss → `otherAudio`, or `call` when `AudioManager.mode` is in-call/ringtone/communication (expo-video's AudioFocusManager pauses with a pending cause), expo-video's own background pause → `locked`, remote → `remote` | `client/patches/expo-video+57.0.5.patch` |
| **iOS/tvOS** (same patch, new `ios/VideoPlayerHealth.swift`): failed items carry NSError `domain`/`code`, the underlying error's domain/code and the last `errorLog()` entry (`domain status comment`) with `httpStatus` (4xx/5xx status or `HTTP nnn` in the comment); `readHealthAsync(withFrames)` (main queue): an `AVPlayerItemVideoOutput` attached to the current item counts new pixel buffers per read (≤ 1 per second — enough for black/frozen, so no `framesDropped` is reported on AVPlayer: the slideshow rule needs real frame counts), `AVPlayerViewController.isReadyForDisplay` of the player's view, enabled item tracks (audioProgress 0 only when video plays with no enabled audio track — the D21 heuristic, never guessed otherwise), access-log `observedBitrate`, `isExternalPlaybackActive` + the AirPlay route's port name; `AVAudioSession` interruption began → `call` (CallKit `CXCallObserver` has an active call) / `otherAudio` / `locked` (`appWasSuspended`), ended with `shouldResume` → `resume`; route change `oldDeviceUnavailable` → `airplayLost` (previous route AirPlay) / `headphones`, reported only when the player really stopped (0.3 s); expo-video's background pause → `locked` | same patch |
| expo-video is a **prebuilt AAR / XCFramework** in SDK 57: the patched sources only compile with `"expo": {"autolinking": {"buildFromSource": ["expo-video"]}}` in `client/package.json` (found live: the first APK had no `readHealthAsync`) | `client/package.json` |
| Engine: `readHealth()` maps the native answer (`native-probe.ts` `toHealth`: numbers/flags only, null → unknown; an unpatched build answers `{}` → no rule runs); `errorReason()` builds `CODE (UNDERLYING) [mime] {errorLog}: message` and passes `httpStatus` as the event `status`; system pauses become `userPlayback {paused, cause}` only when the app wanted playback (a pause the app asked for is never blamed on the OS; `remote` is the viewer's own pause without a system hint); a pause within 2 s after PiP closed is `pipClosed` (A16); the OS's `resume` plays again (end of a call); `isExternalPlaybackActiveChange` → `external {active, device}`; a rejected `replaceAsync` sets `error` (D40) | `client/src/player/engines/{expo-video-engine.tsx,native-probe.ts}` |
| Controller/status (minimal): `userPlayback.cause` → `systemPaused` + `systemCause` (cleared on resume/play), `external` → status hint `airplay {device}` (lowest priority, no actions); `pausedBySystem {cause}` uses the engine's cause, `outside` stays the fallback of the generic pause watcher; a step-down for a decoder failure says the format (`decoder {format}`: HDR for -12927, HEVC/AV1/H.264/Dolby Vision/E-AC-3/AC-3/DTS/TrueHD from the mime) | `controller.ts`, `recovery/status.ts`, `engines/types.ts` |
| classify: `NSURLErrorDomain -1001` → timeout, `NSURLErrorDomain -12xx` → TLS, `-11819` (media services reset) → reclaimed (T6 reload), `ERROR_CODE_IO_CLEARTEXT_NOT_PERMITTED` → T11 `cleartext_not_permitted`, audio sink / audio-format decoder errors → T7 `audio_decode_error`; `decoderFormat(detail)` | `recovery/classify.ts` |
| New codes with de/en card texts (T7 `audio_decode_error`, T11 `cleartext_not_permitted`, T7 `vlc_error`, T7 `vlc_dialog`), inserted after `decoder_reclaimed` (not at the end, to stay clear of the F10-main appends) | `api/error-codes.ts`, `api/error-categories.ts`, `i18n/locales/{en,de}.json` |

## Slice S7 — VLC probes

| Part | Where |
|---|---|
| libVLC patch: `getStats` adds `decodedAudio`, `playedAbuffers`, `lostAbuffers` (libVLC 4.0.0-eap29 `IMedia.Stats`, all `long`) | `client/patches/expo-libvlc-player+57.0.54.patch` |

| `VlcEngine.readHealth()`: frames presented/dropped/decoded, audioProgress = played buffers once audio is decoded (decoded but never played = silence, D25), readyForDisplay after the clock started, has video/audio from the decoders. Reloading at …") | `client/src/player/engines/vlc-engine.tsx` |
| `directRendering: true` (dev player option) decoding ≥ 4 s of clock without a displayed picture reloads once with `:no-mediacodec-dr` at the position (state `loading` so the watchdog waits) | same |
| `onDialogDisplay`: `dismiss()` at once (nobody answers on a TV) + engine error `vlc_dialog <type>: <title> <text>` → certificate text → T1 `tls_error`, else T7 `vlc_dialog` (D27); any other unexplained VLC error → T7 `vlc_error` "VLC could not play this file" (D22) | `vlc-engine.tsx`, `recovery/classify.ts` |

## Slice S4h — the player's words (code + docs, no devices)

Worked 2026-10-05 06:38–06:55 in `../streamarr-f10` on 296aa6b; nothing committed. Every player-facing text was read in
de and en: start card (stepper), the 27 status hints with their causes and actions, the 17 notice variants, the 11
category texts, the 83 codes the player can put on its card, the reason line, the card actions and "What was tried".
Rule per text: what happened → what the app does → what the viewer can do; no jargon; German in du-form with the app's
words ("Wiedergabe", "Version", "Verbindung", "Umwandlung"); short enough for a TV card and a phone banner; numbers
per locale; no two states with one text unless the viewer does the same about them. Keys are unchanged.

| Area | Before | After (en / de) | Why |
|---|---|---|---|
| Jargon | stepper "Fallback" / "Ausweichen", "Choose method", "Start stream"; method label "Transcode"; "without transcoding"; reason "ffmpeg is not installed"; "video decoder was taken away"; release_dead "(and every fallback)" | "Next version" / "Nächste Version", "Choose how to play" / "Wiedergabeart wählen", "Start playback" / "Wiedergabe starten"; "Converted" / "Umgewandelt"; "without conversion"; "The server’s video converter isn’t installed."; "Another app took over the video"; "the versions tried in its place" | words the viewer cannot act on |
| Missing "what you can do" | deviceSlow, endedEarly, autoplayBlocked, subtitleNotDeliverable(+Vlc) | "A lower quality helps." · "Another version may have the rest." · "Press Play." · "Choose other subtitles." · "Play with VLC to see them." | the hint carries the viewer's way out (its action button names it) |
| Order | step-down notice: "Switched to … to keep playing. The video loaded too slowly." | reason first: "The video loaded too slowly. Switched to …" (`overlay-labels.ts`) | what happened, then what the app did |
| Touch words on TV/web | mutedAutoplay "Sound off — tap to unmute." | "Started without sound (browser rule)." + action "Unmute" | "tap" is wrong on TV and web; the button says what to do |
| German | "Wechsel zu …, damit es weiterläuft" (5 variants) next to "… gewechselt, damit es weitergeht"; "Problem auf dem Server." vs "Der Server hatte ein Problem."; "Sie startet neu bei"; "Laden dauert …"; "Das Video lud zu langsam"; "Direktstream"/"Direkt-Stream"; "verlorene Frames" vs "Bilder ausgelassen"; "Versuche es noch einmal" next to "erneut" | one pattern "Zu X gewechselt, damit es weitergeht."; "Der Server hatte ein Problem."; "Neustart bei"; "Das Laden dauert …"; "Das Video kam zu langsam an."; "Direkt-Stream"; "ausgelassene Bilder"; "Versuche es erneut" (playback codes only; "gleich noch einmal" = "in a moment" stays) | consistent words |
| Vague | queued "Waiting for the server."; stream_expired "Stream expired / The stream expired."; playback_not_found "Start it again."; attempt "degraded"; tried.G "Gave up"; audioRestarted "by restarting the stream"; causes "call", "another app plays audio" | "Waiting until the server has room for this video."; "Access expired / The access to this video expired. Start playback again to continue where you left off."; "… to continue where you left off."; "repairable" / "reparierbar"; "Stopped trying" / "Nicht weiter versucht"; "Audio track switched; playback restarted at the same spot for it."; "incoming call", "another app is playing sound" | says what happened and what follows |

Kept on purpose: codec-free "decode" in T7 texts (the viewer's action, VLC or another version, follows from it);
"Direct play / Direct stream" as method names in the info panel; four shared card titles and one shared card message
(listed with the reason in the catalogue, pinned by the test).

**Catalogue.** `docs/client/player/hint-catalogue.md` (285 lines) is generated from the locale files by
`client/jest/player/hint-catalogue.ts` (state, action, shows/clears per row are hand-written there; texts, hint actions,
card actions via `cardActions()` and categories via `categoryOf()` come from the code). Tables: start card S01–S20,
hints H01–H27 (+ causes, hint actions), notices N01–N17, categories T1–T11, codes F01–F83, reason line, card actions,
What was tried W01–W08, shared texts. `client/src/player/__tests__/hint-catalogue.test.ts` (8 tests): the file equals
the generated text (regenerate with `UPDATE_CATALOGUE=1 npx jest hint-catalogue`); every state key of the player
namespace has a row; no jargon (HLS, remux, T-codes, playbackId, ffmpeg, transcode, decoder, fallback, codec, segment,
rendition) in any text; length limits (start 80, hint 130, notice 160, card title 50, card message 200 characters);
shared titles/messages equal the documented list; de/en number formats (2,4 / 2.4 Mbit/s, 42 % / 42%); the step-down
notice order. Before the rewrite the jargon test failed (release_dead "fallback"; the H01 mutation shows it for ffmpeg).

Tests that asserted texts followed: B07 (reason line), D32 (audio restart notice), E12 (paused cause); state-matrix
B07/D32 quotes updated.

| # | Mutation | Result |
|---|---|---|
| H01 | reason line back to "ffmpeg is not installed on the server." | caught (catalogue, jargon, B07) |
| H02 | a German hint changed without regenerating the catalogue | caught (catalogue) |
| H03 | step-down notice back to action-before-reason | caught (notice order) |
| H04 | picture_frozen titled like video_stalled | caught (shared titles) |

**Note on the time label.** The overlay's time label in `innerText` lags the clock by up to 10 s in headless Chrome. Positions in this report are `video.currentTime`.

### FAIL rows — repro and suspected code

1. **SEEK-SUB — every seek / start at a position / reload with subtitles on says the subtitles failed and turns them off for a minute.**
   - Repro: Sintel (`tmdb-movie-45745`) with German subtitles, no fault, `__streamarrPlayer.seekTo(130)`. You get the notice "Untertitel „Deutsch“ konnten nicht geladen werden und sind aus …" at 1 s, and the overlay shows "Aus".
   - The hls.js error on the seek is `{details:'aborted', fatal:false, frag:{type:'subtitle'}}`, which hls.js raises itself when it cancels an in-flight load.
   - Suspect: `subtitleFailure()` in `client/src/player/engines/web-engine.web.tsx:719-728` counts any error whose `frag.type === 'subtitle'`, including `aborted`. It forwards that as `subtitleError` (`:435-436`), and the controller acts on it in `onSubtitleError` (`controller.ts:684-702`).
   - Fix direction: ignore `details === 'aborted'` (and errors raised while seeking or reloading).
   - The same aborted audio fragment feeds item 3.

2. **C12/C18 — a playlist without ENDLIST ends the film early with the end card.**
   - Repro: `{"fault":"playlist_endless","scope":{"next":"anna"},"params":{"segments":10},"mode":"always"}`, then open `/play/new?workId=tmdb-movie-45745&start=40`.
   - The duration shows 1:00 instead of 3:00. At 0:59 the stall becomes "ZU ENDE / Nochmal abspielen".
   - Suspect: `get duration()` (`controller.ts:346-350`) trusts the engine's finite duration (60 s from the cut playlist) over the server's `mediaInfo.durationTicks` (180 s). The S9a D10 fix only falls back to the server when the duration is not finite.
   - Then `nearEnd()` (`controller.ts:1093-1096`) and the stall handler `stall: () => (this.nearEnd() ? this.finish() : this.startStall())` (`controller.ts:299`) finish the title.
   - Fix direction: when the server's duration is known and the engine's is clearly shorter (> 10 s), use the server's for the end decision. Treat a stall more than `END_MARGIN` before the server's end as a stall, not an end.

3. **D29 — a delayed video segment is called "No sound" for 40 s.**
   - Repro: `{"fault":"seg_delay","scope":{"next":"anna"},"target":"video","params":{"ms":25000},"after":{"segment":12},"mode":"always"}`, start Sintel at 5, then `seekTo(130)`.
   - The viewer sees "Kein Ton. Die Tonspur wird neu geladen … [Andere Tonspur]" from 1 s, then a T7 `audio_rendition_failed` reload.
   - Mechanism: the seek aborts the in-flight audio fragment (`aborted`, status 0). Video fragments arrived within the last 15 s (before the seek), so the S9a "audio rendition failure" rule (`web-engine.web.tsx`, the non-fatal audio fragment branch that emits `loadRetry {audio:true}`) classifies it as an audio failure.
   - Fix direction: as in item 1, ignore `aborted`. Count only video fragments that arrived *after* the failing audio request.

4. **R13 — a restart the server leaves in `starting` ends on "Wiedergabe beendet / playback_not_found" after 30 s.**
   - Repro: Sintel playing, then arm `{"fault":"start_hang","scope":{"next":"anna"},"params":{"seconds":600}}` and `{"fault":"playback_gone","scope":{"playbackId":P},"mode":"always"}`.
   - While N waits for the new playback, the old hls.js source plays on from its buffer. When that runs dry, the stall is T5, so the runner runs **Q** (`/switch` on the closed playback) → `playback_not_found` → card.
   - This is the same mechanism as S9a E09; the S9a fix covered only a throwing `engine.load`.
   - Suspect: the N step in `controller.ts`/`recovery/runner.ts` — the old engine is not paused or detached when a new start is requested after T2, and stalls during an active N step are handled as new incidents.
   - Fix direction: stop the old source when N begins (keep the last frame), and let the N step wait for the server's own budget (`start_timeout` at 60 s, B13b) or the step budget.

5. **B13b viewer switch on a slow server — the old picture is dropped at once, then a 2-minute ladder ends on the wrong card, with a VLC button on web.**
   - Repro: Sintel remux playing, arm `{"fault":"transcode_slow","scope":{"playbackId":P},"params":{"readrate":0.2},"mode":"always"}`, then `__streamarrPlayer.setQuality(720)`.
   - The server reports the switch `ready` quickly (segments then come at 0.2× speed), so the "keep the old source on `start_timeout`" path never applies. The engine loads the new source and the picture goes black at once.
   - Ladder: Q (`segment_timeout`) → S → V (`no_more_methods`) → R → card `picture_timeout` "Kein Bild erschienen" at ~110 s.
   - Problems:
     - (a) A viewer switch could keep playing the old source until the new one delivers its first frame.
     - (b) The final card names "no picture" for a server that converts too slowly.
     - (c) The card offers no Retry, and offers **"Mit VLC abspielen" in a browser**: `ladder.ts:248` (T7 → `['otherVersion','useVlc']`), with no platform filter in `screens/player/card-actions.ts` `cardButtons`.

6. **START (and every reload/switch) — bare black or frozen frame with "0:00 / −3:00" and no visible spinner while the controls show.**
   - Repro: the START repro of S9a: `seg_delay {video, 6000 ms, count 2}` (`next`), Sintel `start=90`.
   - From 2 s to 6 s `status.spinner` is true, but the controls are shown, so no spinner is drawn. The clock reads 0:00 / −3:00 instead of 1:30, and the Play button shows Pause (start-5s.jpg). The hint arrives at 6 s.
   - The same label and missing spinner appear in every reload or switch (E18, C10-503 at 14–35 s, P4).
   - Suspect:
     - The status layer hides the spinner while the controls are visible (`screens/player/player-status.tsx`, layout rule 20).
     - The overlay clock uses the engine position (0 while loading) instead of `controller.position` / `loadPosition` (`overlay-labels.ts` / `play-screen.tsx`).
   - Fix direction: show the spinner (or a small spinner in the Play button) while the controls show, and show the target position while loading.

Observations (no verdict of their own):
- E09's "Was versucht wurde" line reads "Der Ton konnte nicht geladen werden". When `playback_gone` closes the session and an audio fragment fails first, the trigger is classified `audio_rendition_failed` (T7 → R) instead of T2. This works here only because the internal error is terminal. With a healthy engine, a gone playback whose audio fragment fails first would go R → A (`/switch audioFallback` on a closed playback). Worth a row test.
- C12 also showed a subtitle failure at the start: the fault cuts the subtitle playlist too, so that one is legitimate.

- **Processes:** the CDP daemon :9333, headless Chrome :9223 (profile `/tmp/ri/s9a2/profile`) and Metro :8083 are stopped. `lsof` shows nothing listening on 8083/9223/9333.

| 1 | **bug** | `engines/expo-video-engine.tsx:173-184`, `:293-296` | `pause()` clears `wantPlay` but not `systemPause`. The controller adopts it even in phase `failed` (`controller.ts:878-884`). | A13 + A03/A05 on iPhone/iPad. A call pauses (`systemPause = call`), and during the call the session ends. `onSignedOut` → `fail()` → `engine.pause()` and the sign-in card. When the call ends, iOS sends `shouldResume`, so AVPlayer **plays with sound behind the card** and `c.paused` turns false. Any `fail()` during a call does the same (T2 gone, card after the budget). Probe P1. | `pause()` sets `this.systemPause = null`. Also ignore `userPlayback {paused:false}` in the controller unless `phase === 'playing'`. **Checked and fine:** threading of the Android probe (listener and read on the main looper), the `pendingPauseCause` guards
(only set while `playWhenReady`), and the iOS observers removed in `deinit` (`[weak self]`, weak owner, no retain cycle). The
iOS `emitIfStopped` 0.3 s and the JS order are fine: the AVPlayer pause arrives first, the cause 0.3 s later, and
`SYSTEM_PAUSE_MS` 1 s does not race. `toHealth` units match the web probe (bits/s, seconds, monotonic counters); an unpatched
build gives `{}`, so no rule runs and nothing crashes. AirPlay `external` turns off the picture and audio rules
(`watchdog.ts:118`). A17 `stopPictureInPicture` runs in `fail()`. The D28 3 s timeout is in place. The S4i `aborted` filter is
correct: in hls.js 1.7.3 `INTERNAL_ABORTED` only comes from `loader.abort()` (self-cancel) and the init-segment mismatch, never
from a network failure (those are `fragLoadError` / `fragLoadTimeOut`), so it hides no real failure. Server duration:
`NaN`, `0` and `Infinity` fall back to the server length when it is known. Viewer-switch restore: `/switch` keeps the
`playbackId`, so `restore()` stops the right playback and nothing is orphaned. The card's VLC filter is in place via
`vlcAvailable()`. The merges lost nothing I could find: 132 codes, no duplicates, every code in de/en, `start_timeout` T5
and `picture_timeout` T7 once each, `decoder` and `airplay` hints present, no engine-kind branch in monitor or recovery.
Patch comments are one line each.

## Summary
### Summary

The native probes are carefully built. Threading, observer lifetimes and the unpatched fallback hold up, and the merges
lost nothing. Two real JS bugs:
- During a call, an app pause (card, sign-out) is undone by iOS's `shouldResume`, so the video plays behind the card.
- A stale `external` keeps "Playing on …" after the engine changes.

The main risks:
- A probe promise that never settles silently turns the whole watchdog off.
- AVPlayer's error-log HTTP status can override a decoder failure.
- Wording: Android TV HDMI "becoming noisy" reads as "headphones disconnected", and every background pause reads as
  "screen locked".
- A16 depends on an event order that is not verified on a device.
- VLC `getStats` runs off the main thread during teardown.
- The frame counter's cost on real Apple TV hardware is not measured.

12 of 28 mutations survived, mostly in the controller's system-pause, switch-back and duration branches and in VLC/D28
details. Gates are green.

```json
{"bugs":["expo-video pause() keeps systemPause: iOS shouldResume after a call plays behind a failure/sign-in card (P1)","controller.external never cleared on engine change: 'Playing on <device>' stays after AVPlayer→VLC (P6)"],"risks":["monitor awaits readHealth without timeout: a hung native probe disables all watchdog rules incl. clock-frozen (P3)","iOS errorLog.last httpStatus (recovered non-fatal 404) overrides decoder errors → T2 instead of step S (P2)","Android TV HDMI/ARC becoming-noisy shown as 'headphones disconnected'","every background pause reported as 'screen locked' (TV Home, app switch)","A16 pipClosed only if PiP-stop precedes the pause; iOS order likely reverse → 'outside the app' (P5)","Exo REMOTE unpause ignored; controller re-pauses; stale wantPlay hides the next system cause (P4)","VLC getStats off main thread races teardown (iOS data race, Android released Media)","AVPlayerItemVideoOutput cost/HDR path unmeasured on Apple TV hardware","decoder hint says 'converting' while step S may go to VLC direct","VLC direct-rendering reload inside readHealth starts the controller stall timeline"],"testGaps":["OS resume after viewer pause (N05)","play() clears systemPause (N08)","VLC readyForDisplay before start (N09)","D28 stop handler cleared (N12)","AVFoundation 403 mapping (N15)","decoder format for audio_decode_error (N18)","duration Infinity with server 0 (N21)","start floor tolerance (N22)","SWITCH_BACK T6 (N23)","remote userPlayback replayed into controller (N24)","system resumed on its own branch (N25)"],"quality":["detachOutput dead in VideoPlayerHealth.swift","framesDecoded produced but read by no rule","native audioUnderruns/errorCode/renderer unused by JS","dead classify rule vlc_video_stalled kept by a test","multi-line JSDoc in jest/player/native.ts","StatusLayout.spinner pass-through; fixed 64 px notice height","controller.ts 1899 lines; system-pause/external wiring could move out"],"mutationChecks":["28 mutations, 16 caught, 12 survived","survivors: N04 N05 N08 N09 N12 N15 N18 N21 N22 N23 N24 N25 (N04 low value)","git diff --stat empty after every restore"]}
```

## Slice S6r — fixes for the native code review (native half)

Worked 2026-10-05 07:32–07:52 in `../streamarr-f10n` (round-i/f10n at 9bd5c63), code only: no device, no Metro,
no native build (S9b measures with the 0a42d0c builds). Not committed. The controller/monitor/classify-text half
(review 2, 3, 9–11, P3, P6) is the JS builder's in `../streamarr-f10`.

| Review item | Fix | Test (fails without the fix) |
|---|---|---|
| 1 / P1 app pause undone by the OS resume | `ExpoVideoEngine.pause()` (and `release()`) clears `systemPause` and the stray-pause mark; `play()` clears both too, so an iOS "may resume" after an app pause, a viewer resume or no system pause at all plays nothing | A13 "an app pause during a call … is not undone" (also covers review 13/14); N05, N08, M17 |
| 4 / P2 decoder failure read as 404 | iOS patch: the error-log status/entry is attached only when the failure itself is a transport error (`NSURLErrorDomain` or CoreMedia -12938/-12660/-12645, on the error or its underlying error); JS `ownFailure()` drops a status/log from any other AVPlayer failure too (old builds) | D18 "an AVPlayer decoder failure keeps no HTTP status …"; `native-probe.test`; M18 |
| 5 TV "headphones disconnected" | `systemCause(cause, Platform.isTV)`: becoming-noisy on a TV/leanback device → new cause `audioOutput` ("sound output changed" / "Tonausgabe gewechselt") | A12 "… a TV output change"; M19 |
| 6 background = "screen locked" | Android: `backgroundPauseCause()` → `locked` only with the keyguard up or the screen off, else `background`; iOS: `appWasSuspended` → `locked` only when protected data is gone (passcode lock), else `background` ("app left" / "App verlassen") | A12 "a background pause without a lock …"; M24 |
| 7 / P5 PiP ✕ in iOS order | a stop while the app wanted to play is remembered; `onPictureInPictureStop` within 2 s of it (player stopped, ready) emits `pipClosed`; leaving PiP while playing invents nothing | A16 "iOS order …"; M20 |
| 8 / P4 remote play paused again | `systemPlayback {paused:false, cause:'remote'}` (Exo REMOTE) is adopted: `wantPlay`, `userPlayback {paused:false}`; the controller then never re-pauses | A13 "a play from the system controls …" (real engine → controller); M23 |
| 12 VLC getStats race | getStats runs on the main queue in both patches; the engine reads no stats once `shutdown()` started (`stopping`, reset on load) | D28 "after a stop the handler is gone … no stats are read during the stop"; M21, N12 |
| 12b DR reload counted as stall | VLC emits a new `reload` engine event before its direct-rendering-off reload; the controller (7 lines) treats it as a fresh load: picture flag off, start budget, stall cleared, new monitor source | D23 "VLC's own reload …"; `vlc-engine.test` asserts the event; M22, M25 |
| test gaps 15/17/18 | readyForDisplay before VLC's clock, AVFoundation 403 (-12660/-1102 → `unknown_stream`), decoder format for `audio_decode_error`, NaN/Infinity in `toHealth` | `native-probe.test.ts` (new), D18; N04, N09, N15, N18 |
| quality | dead `detachOutput()` removed (Swift); `audioUnderruns` (Kotlin), `errorCode`/`renderer` (PlaybackError) no longer sent — nothing read them, the silent rule needs no second counter and the card shows the code name already; `jest/player/native.ts` JSDoc one line; patch comments one line each | — |

## FAIL rows — repro and suspected code
### FAIL rows — repro and suspected code

1. **D36/C20 Exo — a dead audio rendition is called "Connection lost".** Repro: Google TV, Sintel remux, `split_abort {target: audio, rendition: "1", afterBytes: 4096, mode: always}` on the playbackId while the buffer is short (throttle video to 900 kbps first). Exo fails with `ERROR_CODE_IO_NETWORK_CONNECTION_FAILED`. `recovery/classify.ts:118` (`OFFLINE`) makes it T1 `network_unreachable`, so the viewer gets "Connection lost. Retrying in N s…" with backoff 2/4/8/16 s and finally a new start (which only works because the new playbackId escapes the fault). The audio ladder (reload audio → `/switch {audioFallback}`) never runs. Suspected: the expo-video patch reports no track type / URL for the failing load, so the controller cannot tell an audio-rendition failure from a network loss. NetInfo said online the whole time and the video kept loading, so "offline" is contradicted by the evidence.
2. **D41/E14 Exo — one bad segment skips the reload and blames the device.** Repro: `seg_corrupt {target: video, mode: garbage, mode: always}`. `ERROR_CODE_PARSING_CONTAINER_UNSUPPORTED` → T7 `decode_error` (classify.ts:114–115 `DECODER`/`PARSER` order) → step S at once (no reload) → notice "Switched to VLC… This device couldn't decode the video." (status.ts decoder hint). The data was corrupt, the device decoder is fine; with `mode: once` Exo even recovers on its own. Expected (matrix D41): reload once, then step down with an honest reason.
3. **C08/R4 Exo — a slow server ends in "The picture didn't appear".** Repro: `transcode_slow {readrate: 0.5, mode: always}` on the playbackId, then pick 720p. `controller.ts:1216-1223`: the start budget expires on a transcode that is only slow and raises T7 `picture_timeout`. The ladder then reloads, steps down (`no_more_methods`), switches to another version (also without a picture in 30 s) and ends on the picture_timeout card after ~2.5 min. The first 27 s correctly said "The server converts…". Expected: "The server converts slower than playback" and lower quality / keep waiting, never a decoder-style card. Secondary: the switch resumed at 0:59 (the position before Replay + seek to 0:20) instead of 0:20. Seen once; likely `resumePosition`/`lastGoodPosition` not updated by a seek made shortly before the switch.
4. **C12/C18 Exo — endless playlist = false "FINISHED".** Repro: `playlist_endless {segments: 10}` `next:anna`, start at 0:40. Exo reports duration 60 and `ended` at 0:59. `controller.ts:959-969` `onEnded()` compares the position with the **engine** duration (`getSnapshot().duration` = 60), so `position >= duration - 3` → `finish()`. `mediaInfo.durationTicks` (180 s) is ignored there, while the `duration` getter (`:343`) also prefers the engine value. Same root cause as the web C12 FAIL in S9a2.
5. **VLC start at a position (Google TV) — frozen 0:00 frame + "The connection is slow." for ~20 s.** Repro: on a playing remux call `setEnginePreference('vlc')` (or let D41 step down to VLC). VLC presents ~5 frames of 0:00 and stalls. `status.ts:186-193` `slowCause()` says `slowConnection` for any non-transcode/remux method (here direct on loopback). Only the 20 s `picture_timeout` reload gets it playing at the position. Suspected: `engines/vlc-engine.tsx` start-time handling (the seek to the start position right after `Playing`), or the start budget is too long for a direct VLC start that decoded frames and stopped.
6. **C22 Exo — failed subtitles vanish silently.** Known S6m gap ("native engines still emit no `loadRetry`/`subtitleError` events"). Row C22 is closed only for the web engine; on Exo the viewer is never told the German subtitles are off.
7. **A12 on Google TV — "Paused: screen locked."** for a pause that came from an incoming intent (deep link while playing). The expo-video background pause maps to `locked` (patch, S6). On a TV, `outside the app` would be the honest text. Minor.
8. **Cosmetic, all Exo reloads/switches (R5/E18):** while "Playback stopped. Resuming at 0:41…" / "Switching to a lower quality… at 0:41" shows, the picture is the **new source's 0:00/0:01 frame**, not black and not the last frame (gtv-exo-c11-503-21s, gtv-exo-r6-stall-40s, gtv-exo-d36-audio-26s). expo-video shows the first decoded frame before the seek to the position lands. Not counted as a separate FAIL.

## Summary
### Summary

Google TV ExoPlayer handles the transport faults well. Start delay, 404, 503, stall, server-ended playback, truncated and single corrupt segments all recover at the position with specific hints, and audio/subtitle choices survive. The failures are mis-explanations and wrong ladders. An aborted audio rendition says "Connection lost" (D36). A corrupt segment blames the device and jumps to VLC without a reload (D41). A slow server ends in "The picture didn't appear" (C08). An endless playlist ends as "FINISHED" at 0:59 (C12). VLC starts at a position on a frozen 0:00 frame saying "The connection is slow." for 20 s. Native subtitle failures are silent. Only the Google TV AVD was covered in this turn.

Retrying…' 45 s + new start; no 'No sound', no audio conversion (classify.ts OFFLINE)", "D41/Exo: seg_corrupt always -> no reload, immediate step-down to VLC with 'This device couldn't decode the video'", "C08/Exo: transcode_slow + viewer 720p -> picture_timeout ladder -> card 'The picture didn't appear' after ~2.5 min; switch resumed at 0:59 instead of 0:20", "C12/Exo: playlist_endless -> 'FINISHED' at 0:59 of 3:00 (onEnded uses engine duration 60)", "START/VLC (Google TV): VLC start at a position shows a frozen 0:00 frame + 'The connection is slow.' ~20 s until the picture_timeout reload", "C22/Exo: subtitle 404 -> subtitles vanish without a notice (no native subtitleError)", "A12/Exo on TV: an intent-caused activity pause shows 'Paused: screen locked.'"], "evidence": ["docs/client/screenshots/F10/s9b/ (126 JPEGs, prefix gtv-exo-*, gtv-vlc-*)", "/tmp/ri/s9b/ (driver scripts shot.sh, timed.sh, arm.sh, pb.sh, grid.py)", "in-app sampler logs quoted in the table (status/recovery/failure per row)"]}

## Slice S6s — native half of the S9b turn-1 findings

Worked 2026-10-05 07:54–08:15 in `../streamarr-f10n` on top of the uncommitted S6r diff (9bd5c63), code only: no
device, no Metro, no native build. Not committed. The patch parts are proven by the jest fakes until the next
build + live slice. C08 (slow transcode → picture_timeout), C12 (endless playlist → false end) and the "connection
is slow" wording for direct play are the JS builder's.

| S9b item | Fix | Row test (fails without the fix) |
|---|---|---|
| 1 D36/C20 Exo: a dead audio rendition said "Connection lost" | Android patch: `PlaybackError.uri` = the failing request (`HttpDataSourceException.dataSpec`, else the last `onLoadError` URI within 3 s); iOS patch: the error-log `uri` of a transport failure. JS `failureReason()`: a failure whose URL is an audio rendition (`…/audio/{id}/…`, docs/api.md § HLS) while the picture plays and without a server status → `audioRendition:` → T7 `audio_rendition_failed` → the existing audio ladder (reload with "No sound", step A `/switch {audioFallback}`, card). The same failure on a video segment stays T1 | D36 "Exo: a dead audio rendition while the picture plays is the audio path …"; `native-probe.test` (part/playing/status cases); M26 |
| 2 D41/E14 Exo: a corrupt segment blamed the device and skipped the reload | `PARSING_CONTAINER_*`/`ParserException` on a segment URL → `damagedSegment:` → new code `media_damaged` (T7). The ladder treats it as no device failure (the "recurring device failure → straight to another way" shortcut does not apply), so it reloads once at the spot, then steps down. The step-down notice says "The video data is damaged at 0:41." (`notice.because.media_damaged`, with the position: the step-down notice now carries `at`); card "The video is damaged here". Without a segment URL (a direct-play container the device cannot open) it stays `decode_error` | D41 "Exo: a segment that does not parse is damaged content …" (incl. `nextStep(…recurring)` → R); M27–M29 |
| 3 VLC start at a position: frame 0 + ~20 s stall | The libVLC `time` prop stays (M4.2: `:start-time` makes libVLC's clock relative); the VLC surface is covered black until the clock passes the start, and when libVLC plays without reaching the start for 3 s the start seek is sent again (twice at most), also from the probe tick when no time events come | D24 "VLC started at a position: black until the clock passes it, the dropped start seek is sent again after 3 s"; M33, M34 |
| 4 C22 Exo: a subtitle 404 vanished silently | Both patches emit `loadError {uri, trackType, status}` (Exo `onLoadError` with the track type; AVPlayer `newErrorLogEntryNotification` of the current item). The engine maps a text request (track type or `…/subtitles/{n}/…`, `.vtt`) to one `subtitleError` per rendition (`unknown_subtitle_stream` for 404, else `subtitle_unavailable`, as the web engine) → the existing subtitle path (off + notice, retry later); audio/video load errors become `loadRetry {status, audio}` for the stall rules | C22 "Exo/AVPlayer: a subtitle request that fails …"; M30, M31 |
| 5 A12 Google TV: "Paused: screen locked" | `systemCause('locked', tv)` → `background` ("app left") on TV, in addition to S6r's keyguard/screen check | A12 "Google TV: an activity pause from an intent says "app left" …"; M35 |
| 6 Exo/AVPlayer: the new source's frame 0 under "Resuming at …" | The expo-video surface is covered black (`engine-start-cover`, `colors.video`) from a load with a start position until the start seek landed (or gave up); a paused start, a viewer seek or a pause after the seek show the frame at once | `expo-video-engine.test` "start cover (S9b …)" (4 cases); M32, M36 |

**Also:** hint catalogue row N10 (`notice.because.media_damaged`) and the regenerated `docs/client/player/hint-catalogue.md`;
`expo-video-engine.test.ts` → `.tsx` (renders the surface).

**Mutation proof:** M1–M36 all caught (M26–M36 new for S6s); files restored byte-exact (`git diff --stat` unchanged).

Observation, not counted: on the iPhone, the end card's "**Back to details**" did not react to three taps (after the title had played through the end; the player was opened by a deep link, so there was no details screen below it). On the iPad, "Close player" did not react either. These may be the same deep-link / no-screen-below cause or an argent tap problem on iPad. Repro: open `streamarr:///play/new?…` from a cold home screen, play to the end, tap "Back to details".

#### FAIL rows (turn 2) — repro and suspected code

1. **AVPlayer hides segment HTTP errors (C10 404, C11 503).** Repro: iPhone, remux or transcode, `seg_status {target: video, status: 404|503}` on the playbackId. AVPlayer retries quietly and never raises an error. The JS layer sees only a stall, so the ladder runs **Q** (lower quality) with "Loading takes longer…" or "**The server converts slower…**" (`status.ts` `serverSlow`/`buffering` by method). For a gone session (404) it then lands on the **"No picture appeared / picture_timeout"** card, because the stepped-down source of the same dead playbackId never shows a frame (`controller.ts:1216-1223` start budget). Expected: N (new start) for 404, and "Problem on the server, retrying" for 503. Suspected fix area: the patched expo-video iOS probe (S6) gives no `loadRetry`/access-log errors (S6m "Open"). `AVPlayerItem.errorLog()` / `AVPlayerItemNewErrorLogEntry` carry the HTTP status per segment request and could feed `classify.ts` the way the Exo HTTP status does.
2. **Second server loss within the incident window ends on a card (B25, iPhone).** Repro: `playback_gone` once (silent N), then on the new playbackId ~95 s later. The stall goes to **S**, `/switch` answers 404, and the runner stops with "Playback ended — playback_not_found" instead of a second N. Suspected: the runner's per-incident budget for N (`recovery/runner.ts`) is not reset when the earlier N succeeded and played. Also, `playback_not_found` from `/switch` during S should map to N, not end.
3. **D36 audio on AVPlayer:** an aborted audio rendition → stall → "server converts slower" → S (no other method) → **V (another version)**. The viewer loses their subtitle language and never learns it was the sound. Same root cause as 1 (no error detail from AVPlayer). The T7 `audio_rendition_failed` path never triggers on iOS.
4. **C22 on AVPlayer:** a 404 on a WebVTT rendition **stalls the whole playback** (AVPlayer waits for the subtitle segment) and ends on the "No options left" card. This is worse than turn 1's Exo C22, where the subtitles just vanished. Suspected: native HLS subtitle renditions on iOS. Options: the client could detect "stall while the selected subtitle is not empty" and turn subtitles off as the first step, or the server could leave a failing subtitle rendition out (or serve it empty) for AVPlayer.

#### Summary (turn 2)

On AVPlayer, everything that only the heartbeat can see (server restart, lock) is handled cleanly. Everything inside the HLS delivery (404/503 segments, audio rendition, subtitle rendition) reaches the app only as an unexplained stall. The ladder then explains it with the wrong cause ("server converts slower"), lowers the quality for no reason, and in three of five cases ends on a card that names the wrong problem (`picture_timeout`, `no_more_methods`, `playback_not_found`). Apple TV and iPad segment rows can't be reproduced with 3-minute Dev World clips: tvOS/iPadOS AVPlayer buffers the whole clip in seconds. They need a longer clip, or a fault armed before the first segment, e.g. a `next:` `seg_status` with an `after` segment index.

**Left (turn 3):**
- Exo C12 once (S4i duration rule).
- iPhone: `seg_corrupt` once/always, `seg_stall`, C25, C26, D19, D20, D21.
- Apple TV: 404/stall with a `next:` fault armed before the start (or a longer title), "Menu during a recovery", card focus (E10 card).
- iPad: overlay of hints/cards in both orientations, A16/A17 PiP (boot via argent `boot-device force` *before* installing; taps on the player controls failed).
- Server restart once on Google TV/Exo.
- Google TV VLC rows (direct_status 404/503, direct_reset, direct_truncate at start, D25, D27).
- Unverified observation: "Back to details" on the end card / "Close player" on iPad.

## Slice S4l — the JS half of the live native audit S9b turn 2 (code only, no devices)

Worked 2026-10-06 15:24–15:48 in `../streamarr-f10` on 8273020 (S4j+S4k merged with S6r+S6s); nothing
committed. Turn 2 ran 9bd5c63 with the OLD native builds, so AVPlayer reported no segment errors at all. With S6s the
engines emit `loadError` → `loadRetry {status, audio}` / `subtitleError`; the rules below use those, and keep the old
behaviour where nothing arrives. Not touched: the patches, `expo-video-engine.tsx`, `vlc-engine.tsx`, `native-probe.ts`.
Each fix has a row test that failed before it.

| # | Root cause | Fix | Row test |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 C10 404 | a `loadRetry {status:404}` was only remembered for the stall ladder, whose `stallFailure()` turns anything below 500 into T5 → Q (lower quality on a playback the server no longer knows), then R, then `picture_timeout` | the controller handles a video `loadRetry` with 404/410 at once (`recovery/stall.ts` `lostPlayback()`): classified as the media status (T2) → N, a new start at the position; skipped while a step is pending or running | C10 "AVPlayer: a video segment answered 404 while it stalls … a new start at the position at once"; C15 "410 … starts anew at once too"; C10 "more 404 retries while the new start prepares are the same loss" |
| 1 C11 503 | the status layer already says "The server had a problem with this part of the video. Retrying…" while a 5xx retry is recent, and the stall ladder turns a recent 5xx into T6 (R with the server hint); but an unexplained stall of a transcode fell back to `serverSlow` ("converts slower") without any measurement | `stallHint()`'s last fallback is `buffering` ("Loading takes longer than usual"); "converts slower" only for a transcode with a measured conversion rate < 1 (S4g) or a measured server wait (first byte ≥ 2 s and longer than the transfer, the S9a C08 web case); the old C08 row "a transcode slower than real time …" measured nothing and now expects "Loading takes longer" | C11 "AVPlayer: 503 … never 'converts slower', never a lower quality"; C08 "… no measured conversion speed and no error says 'Loading takes longer'" |
| 2 B25 second loss | the T2 rule allowed one N per incident (120 s); a second loss of the NEW playback 95 s later (stall → S → `/switch` 404 → T2) found that N used and ended on the card | `Attempt.playbackId` (the playback a step replaced) and `LadderContext.playbackId`: one N per lost playback, at most 3 per incident (`MAX_LOST_STARTS`); a `/switch` answering `playback_not_found` is a T2 like any other, so it gets its N | B25 "a second server loss 95 s after the first, on the NEW playback, starts anew again …"; control B25 "the same playback lost twice is the card"; B25 (review M18) rewritten: four losses two minutes apart all recover, losses within two minutes get three new starts, then the card |
| 3 D36 languages | step V started the other version with `tracks: null` and only the viewer's stored preferences, so a German subtitle chosen in the player was lost; the notice did not say so | new `player/version-languages.ts`: V sends the viewer's audio language and full subtitle language (`subtitleMode: always`) as start preferences; after the switch the notice adds "This version has no German subtitles." / "… no German audio." when the new version lacks them (`notice.noSubtitleLanguage`, `notice.noAudioLanguage`, catalogue N18/N19) | D36 "step V asks the other version for the viewer's audio and subtitle language"; D36 "… says so: 'This version has no German subtitles.'" |
| 3 D36 audio path | AVPlayer reported nothing; with S6s the audio rendition URI arrives as `loadRetry {audio:true}` / `audioRendition:` and takes the existing T7 audio path (noAudio hint, reload, step A) | — (native half, S6s) | existing D36 rows |
| 4 C22 subtitle stall | a `subtitleError` turned the subtitles off, but a stall that AVPlayer started while waiting for the subtitle kept its 15 s budget and stepped down; with no error at all the ladder never thought of subtitles | `onSubtitleError` restarts the stall's budget; `subtitleStalls()`: on AVPlayer (expo-video on iOS, engine state `buffering` — a frozen clock alone is not AVPlayer waiting) a stall that reaches its ladder with a subtitle rendition selected first turns the subtitles off through the subtitle path (notice, retry in a minute), then the usual ladder if it still stalls; hls.js never | C22 "AVPlayer waits for a subtitle segment that fails: … off first and the stall gets a fresh budget"; C22 "… no error at all: the first step turns the subtitles off"; control C22 "the web engine is never asked …" |
| 5 forced subtitle | `currentSubtitle()` read the engine's selection; AVPlayer shows a forced rendition by itself and reports no selected legible option, so the next ladder step sent `subtitleStreamIndex: -1` and the forced German subtitle was gone | a forced track the server selected stays the viewer's while the engine reports none, unless the viewer turned subtitles off (`subtitlesOff`, set by `pickSubtitle(null)`) | C10 "a forced subtitle the engine shows on its own … survives a ladder step"; control C10 "a viewer who turned the forced subtitle off keeps it off" |
| 5 end card / Close | "Back to details" replaced the route without marking the player as leaving; Close always went `back()` | `leavePlayer(router, {detail, opener})`: with no screen below (no opener) Close and "Back to details" replace with the title's detail, else back; `backToDetails` marks the player as leaving | `player-tv-back.test` "leaving a player that was opened by a deep link" (3 cases) |
